[^90-2]: "Tuchorskie Stare Ol., AGH, Wolsztyn court, the Tuchorza estate, Acta commissionis fol. 161.
[^90-3]: Cf. Rutkowski, Badania nad podziałem dochodów, p. 95.
[^90-4]: AW. Kiełczynek, A. I. In the Trojanowskie Ol. the labour due amounted to 8 days of ploughing at 12 rods of 8 ells each (AW. B. 1037).
[^90-5]: AW. Stare Łoskońskie Ol., B. 305.
[^90-6]: AW. Góździn, A. I.
[^90-7]: AW. Paproć, C. 2, fol. 255 (Lipiec Małe Ol.); Rel. Cost. nr 223, r. 1787, fol. 182.
[^90-8]: ĄW. Stobnickie Ol., B. 991: each of them at every harvest ought to reap thirty ridges, or bind the drawn grain and carry it in mendle.
[^90-9]: Rel. Cost. nr 217, r. 1784, fol. 78, Włościejewskie Ol.: the labour due from each huba they ought to render annually as follows: at the harvest to reap sixty ridges of grain, and just as many ridges to plough in whatever field.
[^90-10]: AW. Czapury, B. 1 a: The little field, however, which lies beside our convent, which they formerly mowed, and which is now sown, they must plough, drag and clear of the crescencya.
[^90-11]: Rel. Cost. nr 218, r. 1785, fol. 221, Boruja; AM. Wieleń, GC. 250, fol. 24, Łokacz Ol.
[^90-12]: Rel. Cost. nr 224, r. 1788, fol. 379.

[p. 91]

the — otherwise very rarely met with — obligation to fell a certain defined quantity of timber for the lord's needs[^91-1].

Closely related to the labour dues were the cartages and the “journeys”. In the first case the Olęders were most often obliged to cart firewood from the lord's forest to the manor, usually[^91-2] twice a year;

at Christmas, Easter and Whitsuntide, in the amount of 1–2 loads[^91-3] of[^91-4] (the so-called festival wood, drzewo świętne)[^91-6]. The cartage could be combined with the performance of certain[^91-5] works, as for instance when the Olęders had the obligation to cart wood already felled 4. More rarely we meet the obligation to cart timber in logs to the sawmill, or else the cartage of a certain number of kopas of the lord's grain from the field to the manorial barn 8. In the second case it was the duty of the Olęders to cart to the towns, usually the larger ones, the products of the lord's husbandry (timber, grain, liquors). The Olęders were obliged to carry out the transport with their own teams[^91-7]. The length of the route was either specified in miles 8, or the town to which the future journeys were to be made was named. The towns most often in question were Czersk, Leszno, Poznań and Wschowa, more rarely Komorniki, Toruń and Żydaczów. Sometimes these journeys could be very distant and consequently burdensome. Thus, for instance, the inhabitants of the Karpickie Ol., situated in the neighbourhood of Wolsztyn, were obliged to make the journey to Toruń once a year, or to pay in its stead 5 czerw. zł.

Among the labour dues an important place was occupied by the so-called tłoki [compulsory ploughing-works] (in the sources almost always in the spelling tłuki; German Bit(t)age, Bit(t)dienste. As the very name indicates, these were labour dues performed in case of sudden need. The tłoki differed from the other labour dues in that they were rendered for a remuneration on the part of the lord, usually in beer, so that these labour dues had the character of hired labour. Nevertheless they were not[^91-8]

[^91-1]: LAW. Krosna, A. Z: At the brickyard they must break up three fathoms of wood from each huba every year.
[^91-2]: Kl. XII 6 V, fol. 36, Króteckie Ol.: AW. Niwka, A. 1; AGH, Wolsztyn court, the Gościeszyn estate, vol. V (kart 334), fol. 33, Stodólsko Ol.; AW. Sikorce, B. 911; AM. Wieleń, C. 250, fol. 22, Szklana Huta; AW. Ludomskie OIl., B. 394; AW. 'Trojanowo Ol., B. 1037.
[^91-3]: AW. Rożnowskie Ol., B. 770: The festival wood, two loads per huba, one from a half, they are to cart away at every feast… 1! Sikorce, ibid.
[^91-4]: AW. Barcinck, B. 1; AW. Dziewczastruga Ul., b. 1U28.
* Np. Kałędek Ol., Q, nr 54. |
[^91-5]: Np. Kałędek Ol., Q, nr 54. |
[^91-6]: AW. Orłowiec, A. 1; AW. Białokoskie Ol., B. I.
[^91-7]: In the Białokoskie Ol. 1 time 12 miles, in the Stare and Nowe Borujskie Ol. and in the Szarki Ol. — 2 times 4 miles (AGH, Wolsztyn court, the Hamer estate, III H. 1, 'Tax Acten, fol. 63–4).
[^91-8]: AGH, Międzychód court, the Białokosz estate, vol. IIL,/ fol. 19, Białokoskie Ol.; AW, Karpicko, A. 1; AW. Niwka, A. 1]; AGH, Wronki court, the Popowo estate, XIX 199, a copy of the charter of 1708; AGH, Rogoźno court, the Wojnowo estate, vol. I, fol. 161, Wojnowskie Ol.

[p. 92]

voluntary labour dues, but bound the settlers just like the other manorial obligations. The obligation to render them was often expressly emphasised in the charter. One may doubt whether the remuneration in beer always constituted a sufficient equivalent for the works performed by the Olęders. Initially, in all probability, the tłoki were rendered in small quantity and only in case of absolutely urgent need. Since the Olęders received remuneration for their work, the tłoki were not regarded as an obligation burdening the Olęders, and sometimes no mention of them was made in the charter. Thus, according to the inventory of the Wieleń estate of 1653, the Olęders who were located in that estate at the beginning of the seventeenth century were obliged to work one day at harvest, and yet in the later charters of 1677 we find no mention of these labour dues. On the whole, however, the obligation of rendering the tłoki is clearly formulated in the charters[^92-1], and the tłoki are even clearly distinguished from the labour dues “for the breaking-in of new land”. The charter for the Jerackie Ol. provides that: “other labour dues (apart from those for the breaking-in of new land and the customary tłoki) the manor ought not to demand of the aforesaid Olęders”[^92-2]. The fixing of the number of tłoki in the charter must be regarded as advantageous for the Olęders, for it protected them against possible abuses on the part of the lord. Most often hand labour was rendered (hence the tłoki were sometimes called corvée [szarwark])[^92-3], but we meet also tłoki rendered with draught teams[^92-4].

Owing to the number of working days being sometimes left undetermined more closely[^92-5], the tłoki formed a transitional form between labour dues defined in quantity and those undefined. Among the latter, performed “when the need arises”, the most important group was formed by the so-called corvée works. These include works at the repair of bridges and dykes (carting sand, laying faggots), at the digging and cleaning of ditches, at the repair of the roads passing through the territory of the community, and at the repair of mills 1 manorial buildings[^92-6].

[^92-1]: Kl. M. Suchylas: The tłoki, such as at the harvest 1 and for a sudden sowing, they shall render without excuse. Similarly Kl. XII O V, fol. 116, Lubaskie Ol.; AW. Milczewskie Ol., A. 1; Rel. Posn. nr 6020, r. 1787, fol. 20, Snowidowo.
[^92-2]: AW. B. 296.
[^92-3]: Rel. Cost. nr 220, r. 1787, fol. 182, Charłub Ol.
[^92-4]: Per huba there was paid: in Ostrów and Borgów (1785) — 126 zł, in Skrzydlewo (1785) — 16
[^92-5]: In the Milczewskie Ol., in Snowidowo.
[^92-6]: AW. Blinek Ol., A. 1; AW. Borkowskie Ol., A. 1; Rel. Posn. nr 489, r. 1751, fol. 69, Dymaczewskie Stare Ol.; AW. Jaromierskie Stare Ol., A. 1; Kat. II, fol. 239; AW. Kielczynek, A. 1; AW. Krosna, A. 2; AW. Łoskońskie Stare Ol., B. 365; AW. Marienwald, B. I; Rel. Posn. nr 038, r. 1791, fol. 602, Róża Ol.; Rel. Posn. nr 620, r. 1787, fol. 27, Snowidowo; AW. Jaromierz B. 4, fol. 78, Ujście Nowe.

[p. 93]

Among other works mention must be made of the obligation to cart timber to the lord's buildings[^93-1] and to shear sheep[^93-2], and finally of assistance at the catching of fish[^93-3] and at the brewing of beer[^93-4]. The Olęders were also obliged to care for the integrity and good state of the boundaries and the boundary marks of the settlement[^93-5], and to guard the lord's forest (to watch it against thieves[^93-6] and to put out fires[^93-7]). It must be noted that all these labour dues, undefined as to quantity, are on the whole met with rarely, and then applied in a moderate way; the defined labour dues prevail.

4. Other burdens in favour of the manor. a. The propination compulsion. In almost all the Olęder settlements the propination compulsion was in force. It was either complete, that is, the Olęders were forbidden on pain of penalty to make any liquors, or to obtain them from anyone but the manor[^93-8], or partial, that is, the Olęders might brew a poorer beer, the so-called cienkusz or konwent (Nachbier)[^93-9], while other liquors they were obliged to take only from the lord's brewery or tavern[^93-10]. On extraordinary occasions (christenings, weddings, revels)

[^93-2]: Kl. M. Suchylas.
[^93-3]: Ibid. and Kl. XII 6 V, fol. 36, Króteckie Ol.
[^93-4]: AW. Krosna, A. 2.
[^93-5]: AW. Białokosz, B. 1; AGH, Wolsztyn court, the Gościeszyn estate, vol. V (kart 334), fol. 35, Stodólsko Ol.
[^93-6]: AW. Borkowskie Ol., A. I: Strangers who should presume to ride into the forest without receipts or any other starostwo token, they are free to seize, and moreover they bind themselves to conduct such persons, with their cart, back to the manor. Similarly: AW, Krosna Ol., A. I (Krosińskie Ol.) and A. 2;
[^93-7]: AW. Stare Łoskońskie Ol., A. 1.

Cost. nr 220, r. 1787, fol. 372, Ostrowo: the farmers are to run the tap as genuinely as possible, to introduce no foreign liquors under an exemplary penalty, but rather to strive for the largest possible outlet of the lord's propination …”. Rel. Cost. nr 191, r. 1756, fol. 94, Świącyńskie Ol.: This too they reserve to themselves, that under penalty no one is to go to another's beer, much less bring foreign liquors into their houses from wherever it may be. Rel. Posn. nr 626, r. 1788, fol. 407, Zakrzewo Ol.: Beer, spirits and whatever is to be found in the taverns for tapping, they ought not to take elsewhere, only from the lord or in the lord's taverns.
[^93-10]: AW. Paproć, C. 2, fol. 257, Różyńskie Ol. Rel. Posn. nr 640, r. 1791, fol. 268, Paproć Pl.: it shall be permitted to them also to make the konwent drink, alias cienkusz, in their houses for themselves 1 their household, yet nevertheless, should they wish to arrange a wedding or christenings and other honourable revels and need beer for that, they must take it from my malt-kiln. Item: Rel. Posn. nr 435, r. 1742, fol. 230, Chełmskie Ol.; ibid. nr 645, r. 1793, fol. 101, Komorowskie Ol.; AW. Koziclaski, A. 1; AGH, Poznań court, Naramowice, t. 1, fol. 5; Rel. Posn. nr 043, r. 1792, fol. 54, Sękowo Ol.; ibid. nr 641, r. 1792, fol. 824, Węgielna Ol.

[p. 94]

they were always obliged to drink the lord's liquors[^94-1]. Where the Olęders had the freedom of making liquors, it was usually restricted to production for their own needs, possibly for sale within the settlement[^94-2]. In isolated cases the Olęders enjoyed an unrestricted right of making and selling liquors[^94-3], usually, however, for a separate annual fee, and that usually in money[^94-4], exceptionally in malt[^94-5]. To the earliest-located Wieleń Olęders, who at first could brew beer for their own needs without any fees, there was imposed in the eighteenth century the obligation to buy 5 barrels of beer from the manorial brewery[^94-6]. The propination compulsion also covered spirits[^94-7].

From the propination compulsion, which restricted consumption to the lord's liquors, while the purchase of liquors nevertheless depended on the will of the settlers, there must be distinguished the compulsion to buy a certain definite quantity of liquors, that is, indirectly, a compulsion of consumption[^94-8]. Such a compulsion we meet rarely in the Olęder settlements, and in a mitigated form. Thus, for instance, in the Ol. Sikorca an Olęder arranging a christening or a wedding was obliged to take a barrel of beer at the normal rate[^94-9]. Here one would also have to include the obligation, mentioned above, of buying the lord's beer in return for exemption from the propination compulsion. Similarly there would have to be classified the cases in which the purchaser of an Olęder farm was obliged to take a barrel of the so-called introductory beer[^94-10].

If there was not a sufficient quantity of liquors in the lord's tavern or malt-kiln, the Olęders could obtain them elsewhere, usually with the knowledge[^94-11] or even with the consent[^94-12] of the manor.

Posn. nr 430, r. 1741, fol. 234) the fee was included in the rent, likewise in the Szklana Huta and Wizana Ol. (AM. Wieleń, G. 250, fol. 22–3).

[^94-8]: Cf. Rutkowski, Badania nad podziałem dochodów, p. 197.
[^94-9]: AW. Sikorce, B. 911.

[^94-11]: Rel. Cost. nr 174, r. 1739, fol. 1120, Popowo,
[^94-12]: AW. Góździn, A. I.

[p. 95]

b. The milling compulsion. We meet it in the Olęder settlements only exceptionally[^95-1]. On the whole the Olęders might grind wherever it pleased them.

Other burdens were not laid upon the Olęders; in one settlement only[^95-2] every Olęder was obliged, in the period of Lent, to buy half a kopa of herrings from the lord's tavern. It is not visible, however, whether or to what extent the lord[^95-3] drew profit from this sale.

c. Manorial servitudes on the Olęders' land. In a few cases the lord had the right of pasturing his cattle on the Olęders' land 3. There thus belonged to him, in relation to the Olęders, a certain kind[^95-4] of pasture servitude. It is an understandable thing that, in view of the meagre extent of the Olęders' land, this privilege played no great part in the lord's husbandry.

5. The evolution of the manorial obligations. From the foregoing considerations it follows that the manorial obligations of the Olęders changed in the course of the colonisation. These changes might manifest themselves in this way, that either at the location of later settlements arrangements were made for services different from those in the earlier settlements, or else in one and the same settlement the lords altered the hitherto existing obligations in the course of time. The question of the evolution of the Olęders' obligations has already repeatedly been the subject of consideration in German literature. For the most part the matter has been treated in a simplified 1 one-sided way. Certain extreme cases were cited, and were then generalised. Among the first (in 1798) to concern himself with this matter was Stenger, a Prussian judge at Kargowa and later at Wolsztyn 4. He was struck by the considerable number

[^95-1]: AW. Sikorce, B. 911: Let no one anywhere carry grain, only here to the millers of this estate, and that under a penalty of two thalers should anyone be convicted. And as he must take care that the millers do not loiter at the mills, should they not grind his grain within three days, it shall be permitted to him for those times to take his grain from the mill and cart it wherever it may please him. Apart from this the milling compulsion we meet only in the settlements of the Wieleń estate (according to the charter of 1677), then in Kiszewko (AW. B. 339) and Sowicjgórze (AW. B. 2, fol. 2).
[^95-2]: AW. Kiełczynek, A. 1. It is possible that the profits from this were drawn exclusively by the innkeeper, in which case we should here have to do with a burden upon the settlers not in favour of the lord but of third persons within the communal community.
[^95-3]: AW. Trojanowo Ol., B. 1037: …(sheep) it will be permitted to them to pasture in the Trojanowo forest, likewise it will be permitted to the Trojanowo shepherd to pasture on the Olęders' hubs, without damage, however, to both parties. Similarly: Rel. Posn. nr 640, r. 1791, fol. 248, Rataje Ol.; AW. Szklarka Trzcielska, A. 1, Szklanohuckie Ol.
[^95-4]: Die Haulinder s. 251: ,,...da es denn auch nicht an unberufnen Dienern, weltlichen und geistlichen Standes, fehlte, die ihnen zu Munde redeten, so liessen sie es beinah' jedes mał auf das ilusserste ankommen, ohne zwischen guten und bosen HerrschafIten einen Unter- Schied zu machen. Fs steht mir besonders ein Biespiel lebhaft vor, wo zwey grosse Hauldnder- Gemeinen ihren Herrn verklagten, die nur 10 Tage jahrlich dienten, recht wohlhabend sind und selbst cingestehen miissen, dass sie den besten Herrn von der Welt haben. Dennoch ver- tolgten sie ihn mit einer ausgesuchten Bosheit, stellten sich blutarm, und dichteten ihm Hand- , ą PY lungen an, deren nur der ganz bóse und gemeine Tyrann fahig gewesen seyn wiirde”. ["...for it was not lacking in unauthorised servants, of secular and ecclesiastical estate, who spoke into their ears; and so they almost always carried matters to the extreme, without making any distinction between good and bad lords. A particular example stands vividly before me, where two great Hauländer communities sued their lord, who rendered only ten days of service yearly, are quite prosperous and must themselves confess that they have the best lord in the world. Nevertheless they persecuted him with a refined malice, represented themselves as destitute, and imputed to him deeds of which only the utterly wicked and base tyrant would have been capable."]

[p. 96]

lawsuits for the unlawful imposition or raising of obligations, which the Olęders began to bring against their lords after the occupation of the country by Prussia. Reflecting on their causes, he comes to the conviction that in some cases the lords did in fact violate the rights guaranteed to the Olęders in the charters; on the other hand he does not spare bitter words for the settlers, ascribing to them malice and litigiousness. He also justly draws attention to the unclear and dispute-provoking drafting of many charters. Similarly, a few years later, the Prussian official Holsche treats the matter[^96-1]. He states, however, that the lords, probably in order to entice the settlers, assigned them a very low rent, the real value of which moreover continually diminished[^96-2].

The author of the brochure Uber Ursprung u. Verbreitung des Deutschtums im Lande Posen (1849) accuses the lords of arbitrariness and of failure to keep the provisions of the charters, but admits that in some settlements the Olęders, by way of compensation, ploughed in for themselves the neighbouring lands, or extended the scope of the servitudes, which in the result gave rise to numerous lawsuits[^96-3].

Schmitt treats the matter in an extreme way[^96-4]. He calls the charters “Germanic immunities”, which did not correspond to the “Polish system”. According to him the settlers were oppressed in every way, even a regular serfdom being imposed upon them. The charters, even the royal ones, were as a rule not respected. As an example the author cites the case in which the settlers of the Ujście starostwo were, at the starosta's order, flogged because they gathered hay from the starostwo meadows. The author does not know, however, that in the neighbouring state domain of Podstolice already in Prussian times the domain officials flogged the Olęders of Brzekiniec, and that the latter in petitions to the Prussian king begged earnestly that the rights which they had enjoyed under Polish rule be restored to them[^96-5]. To just the same

[^96-1]: Geographie u. Statistik, s. 492: „Diesc Haulinder hatten oft keine Kontrakte, oder cs war doch darin der Ort, wo sie raden, die Strecke, die sie urbar machen, und die Summe, wleche sie an Abgaben zahlen sollten, nicht genau bestimmt: .es entstanden daher gewóhn- "lich nach Verlauf einiger Jahre zwischen den Haulindern und den Grundherrschaften Pro- zesse, die sich damit cndigten, dass erstere verarmten, ihre Besitzungen verliesscn, in andere Giiter zogen, von necuem radeten und brannten...”. ["These Hauländer often had no contracts, or at any rate the place where they were to settle, the area they were to bring under cultivation and the sum they were to pay in dues were not precisely determined; hence after the lapse of some years lawsuits usually arose between the Hauländer and the landlords, which ended in the former becoming impoverished, abandoning their holdings, moving to other estates, settling and burning anew..."]
[^96-2]: Netzdistrikt, p. 222.
[^96-3]: Uber Ursprung, p. 31 ff.
[^96-4]: Geschichte des Deutsch-Croner Kreises, p. 139.
[^96-5]: In the letter of 23 October 1778 (AW. Brzekiniec C. 1, fol. 68) the Olęders of the Brzekinieckie Ol. and the inhabitants of Brzekiniec complain: 1. that they are unlawfully burdened with manifold transport duties and are subject to continual military requisitions (among others they are forced to make journeys with building material to the fortress of Grudziądz), 2. that instead of remuneration from the officials of the domain in Podstolice (Amt Podstolitz) they receive blows with rods, 3. that their rents, settled by charters from Polish times, are being unlawfully raised. By letter of 28 December 1778 min. Gandi

[p. 97]

petitioned, moreover — adduced by the author as an example — the Olęders of the Ujście starostwo[^97-1]. The fact that in the domain of Podstolice the Prussian officials applied flogging to the Olęders does not prove that it was so in all the domains, but it shows to what errors the method of generalising from extreme cases leads. Brandenburger[^97-2] and Koerth[^97-3] approach the question in a similar way. The reasoning of the latter author proceeds by strange paths. He reproaches the lords with acting contrary to the provisions of the charters, and then, as an example, cites a dispute with the lord over pasture servitudes, adding, however: in the king's name he orders the petitions of the communities to be examined favourably and categorically forbids the complainants to be beaten. Evidently, however, the position of the settlers did not much improve, for on 22 May 1782 the Brzekiniec Olęders send a new letter (fol. 130) to the king of the following tenor: “Nachdem Ihro Majestät aus besonderer Gnadenbezeigung gerichtet haben, uns allergnädigst bey unserer alten Gerechtigkeit, so wie ehemals unter Königlich Pohlnischer Regierung genossen, zu schützen und unverändert dabei zu erhalten; wir aber demohngeachtet nach der Maassgabe des Conducteurs Eichmann, welcher unsere Felder vor zwey Jahren gemessen, in unsern Abgaben sehr erhöhet worden, indem gedachter Conducteur aus unsern Gründen weit mehr gemessen als wir nach altem Königlich Pohlnischen Maasse wirklich besitzen. Da doch unsere Gründe vor wenig Jahren noch durch die ehemalige Pohlnische Obrigkeit aufs allergenauste gemessen wurden und zwar jeden Morgen zu 300 Ruthen, jede Ruthe zu 8 Ellen, jede Elle zu 24 Zoll, die Baustelle wurde aus einem Morgen, darzu Graben, Wege und Triften Zinsfrey gelassen. ["Whereas His Majesty, out of a special mark of grace, has been pleased most graciously to protect us in our old rights, such as we formerly enjoyed under the Royal Polish government, and to preserve them unchanged; we nevertheless, in accordance with the measure of the conductor Eichmann, who measured our fields two years ago, have been very heavily raised in our dues, inasmuch as the said conductor measured far more out of our lands than we actually possess according to the old Royal Polish measure. And yet our lands were still measured a few years ago by the former Polish authority with the utmost exactness, namely every Morgen at three hundred rods, every rod at eight ells, every ell at twenty-four inches, and the building site was released from one Morgen rent-free, together with the ditches, roads and driftways."]

Darnachst wurde uns zu Pohlnischen Zeiten gestattet freyes Bau-Holz, sowohl zu Reparaturen als auch zu gantz neuem Bau von der Königlichen Heide zu holen, welches uns auch bisher von Ihro Majestät unserm allergnädigsten Könige zwar noch nicht ist gänzlich abgeschlagen, dennoch aber bis dato noch keines erhalten haben, so höchstnöthig es auch bey vielen unserer Einsassen, deren Gebäude alt und baufällig ist, zu gebrauchen wären. Wir sind überzeugt, dass Ihro Majestät, unser allergnädigster König und Herr, uns Landes-Väterlicher Hulde geruhen werden, diese unsere Bitte, so wir denenselben in tiefster Ehrfurcht und Veneration übergeben, allergnädigst von uns auch annehmen werden, und uns in obbemeldten Puncten laut unserm Privilegio bey unsern alten Rechten und Schützen behalten werden, welche uns, seitdem unsere Gründe durch obbemeldten Conducteur gemessen worden, dessen Maassgabe wir unmöglich vor richtig annehmen und erkennen können, sind gesindert und unsere Abgaben erhöhet worden, unangesehen das unsere Gründe schlecht und im gantzen hiesigen Amte den sandigsten und untragbarsten Boden gaben, wenn wir bey Ihro Majestät unserm allergnädigsten Könige die hohe Gnade erhalten, werden wir uns verpflichtet halten dero Leutseligkeit und Gnadenbezeigung lebenslang zu rühmen, und dabei unveränderlich zeigen das wir seyn Eur. Königl. Majestät allerunterthänigste Knechte. Bracknitzer Holl., d. 22-ten May 1782”. ["After that, in Polish times, we were permitted to fetch free building timber, both for repairs and for entirely new building, from the Royal Heath; this has indeed not yet been entirely refused to us by His Majesty our most gracious King, but up to the present we have received none of it, however necessary it would be for use by many of our inhabitants, whose buildings are old and dilapidated. We are convinced that His Majesty, our most gracious King and Lord, will deign to show us a fatherly grace, will most graciously accept this our petition, which we submit to the same in deepest reverence and veneration, and will keep us, in the points named above, according to our charter, in our old rights and protections, which — since our lands have been measured by the aforesaid conductor, whose measure we can by no means accept and recognise as correct — have been altered, and our dues raised, notwithstanding that our lands are poor and give, in this whole Amt here, the sandiest and most unprofitable soil. If we retain the high grace of His Majesty our most gracious King, we shall hold ourselves obliged to praise your gracious kindness and mark of grace all our lives long, and therein to show unchangeably that we are Your Royal Majesty's most humble servants. Bracknitzer Holl., dated the twenty-second of May, seventeen eighty-two"]

TóT olędrzy

In the petition to the Prussian king of 1777 the Olęders state: „...ver- móge 3 Privilegiis, welche aul mchreres dcuten, doch nicht anders, als nur auf 2] Hulen ver- wiesen, und auch bey Pohlnischen Zeiten niemahls mehr verzinnset, nunmechro aber hey der vorgewesten Commission Ulassificationis aul 25 Gulen festgesetzet worden”. ["...by virtue of three charters, which mean more than one thing but nevertheless refer only to two and a half Hufen, and were never taxed higher even in Polish times, but have now been fixed by the former Commission of Classification at twenty-five Gulden"]. The lawsuits of the Olęders with the State treasury drag on for several dozen years, into the nineteenth century (G. 1–12).

[p. 98]

“It seems, however, that this right of pasturage was not granted at the location of the settlement, but only acquired through old usage.” He also cites further lawsuits with the Olęders, however already from Prussian times. —

Schmidt takes the same road[^98-1]. As an example of the development of conditions in Wielkopolska he cites the settlements of Nowe Dwory and Folsztyn in the Wieleń estate. According to him the rent in the first settlement was raised from 750 zł in 1677 to 3187 zł in 1773[^98-2], and in Folsztyn in the year

599 zł 12 gr even to 6588 zł. This last figure would be weighty proof if it were true. The author, however, probably by mistake, cited, instead of the amount of the rent, the sum of the settlement's gross income, calculated by the Prussian officials for fiscal purposes[^98-3]. This sum has nothing in common with the Olęders' rent, though it is 2½ times greater. The rent amounted to 2627 zł. Schmidt also failed to mention that in the meantime the Olęders in Nowe Dwory ploughed in for themselves 5 2/5, and in Folsztyn 4 3/5, włókas[^98-4], nor that in Nowe Dwory the włóka contained 60 morgs[^98-5], while in Folsztyn 50 morgs. After taking this into account there falls per włóka: in Nowe Dwory 88½ zł, and in Folsztyn 82 zł 23 gr. On the normal 30-morg włóka we shall obtain respectively:

in the first case 44¼ zł, in the second 49 zł 18 gr. Meanwhile the author states on the following page that the average rent per włóka at that time had settled by itself at 70 to 80 zł. Similar results we shall obtain when we compare the real value of the rents given above. At the beginning of the seventeenth century, when there was assigned to the Olęders the rent still binding in 1677, at the rate of 25 and 22 zł per huba, the silver content in the złoty amounted to 20, a little later 17; meanwhile in the złotys current at the moment of the first partition it amounted to only 3 g[^98-6]. The value of money thus fell 6-fold, while the rent was raised 3–4 times. On the other hand the author quite rightly emphasises that various fees were unlawfully, contrary to the charter, imposed on the Wieleń Olęders, such as solne, stróżowe, popielne, karczunkowe (Reuthergeld), amounting to 3% of the proper rent. These fees, however, were only an episode in the history of the settlements, for, imposed on the Olęders shortly before the first partition, they were after a few

[^98-2]: Kl. XII 7 fol. 170–191, Folsztyn, fol. 229 ff., Nowe Dwory.
[^98-3]: Ibid., fol. 191.
[^98-4]: In consequence the area of land subject to rent increased in Nowe Dwory from 30 to 36 włókas, and in Folsztyn from 27¼ to 31¾ włókas.
[^98-5]: In 1773 the Classification Commission could not establish the actual number and size of the włókas in the settlement; nevertheless the rent per włóka in 1677 being higher by 3 zł than the rent in Folsztyn (where the włóka counted 50 morgs), together with the remarks in the classification records that according to the newer measurements 72 włókas were reckoned in the settlement, indicate that, as in Herbardowo, the włóka here counted 60 morgs.
[^98-6]: Gumowski, Podręcznik numizmatyki, pp. 290–2.

[p. 99]

years annulled; it is not even known whether the Olęders actually paid them[^99-1] in full.

The author's charges concerning the so-called tłoki rest on an obvious misunderstanding. As has already been emphasised, these labour dues never had a voluntary character, and their German name Bit(t)age derives[^99-2] from the word Bede — tax, tribute. The author did not here avail himself of the results of Fr. Guradze's work, which exhaustively explained the matter several years before[^99-3]. In this light the arguments both of Schmidt and of later authors[^99-4] about the oppression of the Olęders through the conversion of the tłoki from a voluntary into a compulsory labour due must be regarded as pointless. Of cases in which the promised payment in beer was refused for the tłoki we have found none in the sources.

A proper view of the development of the manorial obligations of the Olęders is possible only when a broader source basis is taken into account. As regards the rents, their amount, as has already been shown above, rose from 20 zł at the close of the sixteenth century to 100 tymfs in the pre-partition period, that is, 5-fold. Meanwhile the silver content, which in the złoty according to the mint ordinance of 1580 amounted to 20 g., diminished in the tymf to 1.97 g., that is, 10-fold8. In reality[^99-5] the differences were still greater, for in those settlements in which the rents rose at all, the rise was always belated in relation to the fall in the value of money. Thus, for instance, in the Wieleń settlements cited above the rent remained the same from the beginning of the seventeenth century to 1677, although in that period the value of money had fallen by more than half. Moreover the price of land rose as its supply dwindled, and in the same way the value of the pastures 1 forests, and hence of the servitudes, increased. In the Stróżewskie Ol., for instance, the average price of a one-huba farm, 60 thalers about 1740, rose to 200

[^99-1]: Probably by a judicial verdict. In any case, according to the inventory drawn up shortly before 1800 (AM. Wieleń, G. 250 fol. 20) the obligations of the Olęders consist only of rents (3030 zł 20½ gr in Nowe Dwory and 2349 zł 28½ gr in Folsztyn) and of the labour dues which they were already rendering in 1653.
[^99-2]: Cf. J. u. W. Grimm, Deutsches Wórterbuch, under the words Bede and Bitte,
[^99-3]: Der Bauer in Posen, s. 296: „Ab und zu bestanden , „Bittdienste” (tłuki) von 3—30 Ścharwerkstagen. Dies sind wohl nicht ,,urspringlich freiwillig geleistete”, sondern vermehrte Dienste zur Erntezeit, in Siidpreusscn oft Gewaltdienste (gwałty) genannt. Man denke an den Ausdruck ,„Bete” oder Bede fiir Steuer, wo doch auch eine urspringliche Freiwilligkeit der Leistung kaum vorliegen kann”. ["From time to time there existed 'Bittdienste' (tłoki) of three to thirty corvée days. These are probably not 'originally voluntarily rendered' services, but increased services at harvest time, often called in South Prussia Gewaltdienste (gwałty, forced works). Let one think of the expression 'Bete' or Bede for tax, where an original voluntariness of the service can hardly have obtained either."]
[^99-4]: Cf., among others, Schulz W., Die zweite deutsche Ostsiedlung, p. 59. Only Schulz C. (Quellen u. Urkunden, I, p. XV) objectively draws attention to the sentence quoted in the preceding note, regarding the origin of the tłoki as unclear.
[^99-5]: The year 1597, Ol. Ujskie. 6 Gumowski, Podręcznik numizmatyki. 7%

[p. 100]

thalers in 1773 (cf. the community book), and it must be remembered that in the meantime the value of the thaler had diminished only slightly. In the later settlements dues in kind and labour dues also appear. As Kniat has shown, who on the basis of the classification records of 1773 investigated and presented statistically the manorial obligations[^100-1] of the rent-paying settlements of the so-called notecki district, which were mostly Olęder settlements, the dues in kind and the labour dues of these settlements, converted into cash, amounted to 5–30% of the sum of the rent. If we assume that certain fees, e.g. for the servitudes and others, may have been included in the rent, the total value of the dues in kind, labour dues[^100-2] and fees will still not exceed 10% of the rent sum. These results find confirmation in other sources[^100-3]. Of course there are[^100-4] localities in which the dues in kind and the labour dues have a considerably greater value in relation to the rent, but there the rent is lower than the average given by us[^100-5]. In the final conclusion it must be stated that the increase of the manorial obligations of the Olęders was as a rule only nominal, while the real value of the obligations on the whole diminished. This fact will stand out still more sharply if we compare it with the process of the continual increase of the burdens of the enserfed population 5. The legal-moral aspect of the question presents itself somewhat differently. Between the lord and the Olęders disputes sometimes arose, which after the occupation of the country by Prussia expressed themselves in a whole series of lawsuits. The complaining party was most often the Olęders, although it was sometimes the reverse[^100-6]. Three basic causes of the lawsuits may be distinguished: a) the unclear formulation of the obligations 1 rights of the Olęders in the charters, b) the desire for profit or ill will on the part of the lord, c) the same on the part of the Olęders.

[^100-1]: The notecki district comprised the part of the Poznań county taken in the first partition.
[^100-2]: Kniat, Gospodarcze znaczenie ciężarów, pp. 38–41.
[^100-3]: Cf., e.g., AGH, Pnicwy court, the Psarskie estate, Tąx Acten vol. IL, Psarskie Wielkie i Małe Ol.; Rel. Cost. nr 217, r. 1784, fol. 429, Pecna Ol.; AGH, Międzychód court and estate, vol. IT, fol. 195, Drzewce Ol.
[^100-4]: E.g. AW. Dziewczastruga, B. 1029; AGH, Rogoźno court, the Wełna estate, vol. LI, fol. 297, Jaracz Ol.; AGH, Międzychód court, the Białokosz estate, vol. I fol. 16 and vol. IT, fol. 19, Białokoskie Ol.
[^100-5]: Rutkowski, Zarys dziejów, p. 150 ff. Zimmermann (Fryderyk Wielki, II, p. 306) emphasises this in a forceful form: travellers visiting Poland were struck by the difference in the position in which the German peasant stood in relation to the Polish one. The former was privileged in every respect; the latter, lawless, unworthy of a human being, led his life. The former was happy and grew fat on the Polish soil; the latter could scarcely retain enough that he might not die of hunger.
[^100-6]: Two such cases from Polish times we have adduced on pp. 39 and 69. Already in Prussian times Melchior Łącki brings an action against the Olęders of Węgielna for compensation for oaks unlawfully felled, belonging to the manor (AW. Węglewo, C. 1).

[p. 101]

The unclear, often ambiguous content of the charters created in many cases an occasion for disputes and litigiousness. In the charter for the Cisiogórskie Ol. we find, for instance, the following, unclearly drafted provision: “At the harvest they are to help in the work according to need as the other Olęders shall be; in law, however, so many days shall be expressed as is the custom of the Olęders … Wood for the lord's need, as also for the kitchen, they are to cart like the other Olęders 1”. It is an obvious thing that “the custom of the Olęders” can be interpreted very widely. Elsewhere the Olęders are to present themselves for work at call, and at the harvest they are to labour as long as until everything has been cleared away[^101-2]. The scope of the servitudes was likewise unclearly defined; e.g. the provision: “sheep[^101-1] and other cattle they shall keep in moderate number, so that the lord's livestock may have their ease” 3, directly encourages litigiousness.

Disputes arising from the desire to raise the Olęders' hitherto existing obligations and to restrict their rights form a serious percentage of all the conflicts of the lord with the Olęders. The pretensions of the manor, although for the most part, as has been shown above, economically justified, often stood in contradiction to the promises given to the Olęders in the charter[^101-3]; hence, from the legal point of view, they must be qualified negatively. This matter, as appears from the utterances of the contemporary Prussian judge Stenger, caused the courts great trouble, which, although it stood on the ground of the complete respect of the provisions of the charters, could not here fail to notice the discord between law and equity[^101-4]. There occurred also, though rather rarely, demands of the manor bearing already the stamp of exploitation. To such, for instance, belonged the additional fees mentioned by Schmidt (p. 382), imposed by the Szpiech family on the Olęders of the Wieleń estate. Similarly in 1791 Ignacy Bniński tried to impose new charters on the Stare Jaromierskie Ol. and the Stare 1 Nowe Ujskie Ol., in which[^101-5] he clearly raised their hitherto existing obligations and at the same time established hereditary sołtys[^101-6]. In the Wielkie Budziszewskie Ol. the Tomicki family twice, in 1759 and 1770, raised the services and added labour dues. The Prussian councillor v. Zacha, contrary to the charter, considerably raised[^101-7]

[^101-3]: AW. Karpicko, A. 1.

V fol. 146). In the end the court took up an intermediate position, and the costs were borne by the parties in equal halves.

[^101-6]: AW. Jaromicrz, B. ].

[p. 102]

in 1793 the obligations of the Strzeleckie Ol. l. Likewise in the Ol. Dziewczastruga[^102-1] in 1796 the obligations were raised out of all proportion by the steward of the Wełna estate, v. Brauneck (the owner was Goetzendort Grabowska). On the whole, however, cases of a real increase of the Olęders' obligations occurred rarely[^102-3]. At the moment of the enfranchisement[^102-2], as appears from the mortgage records, in the great majority of cases the obligations fixed in the location charters were binding. This is expressly emphasised in the reces for the Stodólsko Ol. 4: “Die Wirthe,[^102-4] welche aus der Stelle der Stiftungs-Urkunde „Jeżeliby zaś od kogo mieli mieć jaką oppressyą etc” die Verbindlichkeit der Gutsherrschaft zum Schutz und zur Vertretung ihres Eigenthums herleiten wollten, erkennen nunmehr an, dass sie im vollständigen Besitze der Ihnen vermöge der Stiftungs-Urkunde verliehenen Grundstücke und Rechte sind, und dass der ihnen verheissene Schutz und die Vertretung daher erfüllt, und nicht weiter zu beanspruchen sind”. ["Those farmers who wished to derive from the wording of the foundation charter 'should they however suffer any oppression from anyone, etc.' an obligation of the manor to protection and to the representation of their property, now recognise that they are in full possession of the lands and rights granted to them by virtue of the foundation charter, and that the protection promised them and the representation are thereby fulfilled and are not to be claimed further."]

Exceptionally it even happened that the lords diminished the Olęders' obligations in later charters, as was the case in the Chełmskie Ol.[^102-5], and probably also in the Lubaskie Ol. 8. It must finally be noted that the relations presented above cannot be an expression of the “Polish system”, as Schmidt would have it, if only for this reason that the real economic oppression of the Hollanders was initiated a whole 200 years earlier by Albrecht Hohenzollern in East Prussia[^102-6].

The causes of some of the lawsuits must also be sought in the craftiness and ill will of the Olęders. As has already been stated above, even German literature admits that the cause of many lawsuits was the unauthorised extension by the Olęders of their original servitude rights and the ploughing in of land[^102-7], which in the Olęder settlements occurred notoriously. It is also known that Frederick the Great represented his invasions

[^102-2]: AW. Dziewczastruga, B. 1029.

To what extent this opinion misses the truth is shown by annex I, in which there is enumerated a whole series of settlements located precisely in the second half of the eighteenth century. 4 AGH, Wolsztyn court, the Gościeszyn estate, vol. V (kart 334), fol. 35.
[^102-4]: Rel. Posn. nr 435, r. 1742, fol. 230 and nr 503, r. 1753, fol. 183. The rent, which in 1732 amounted to 12 thalers per huba, was in 1740 reduced to 10 thalers.

He does not, however, give the content of the provisions of the first charter. (Kl. XII 6 V, fol. 110).
[^102-6]: Schumacher, Niederlindische Ansiedlungen, s. 37, 93 ff.
[^102-7]: Cf. Uber Ursprung, p. 31 ff.

[p. 103]

on Wielkopolska as manifestations in defence of the German colonists, and the plunderings of the Prussian troops as a restitution of their property[^103-1]. It is clear that this had to implant among the Olęders, who were in great part Germans, especially after the catastrophe of the partitions, the conviction that special, far-reaching consideration was due to them from the Prussian authorities. Without entering into whether and to what extent they really profited by these considerations, the lawsuits brought by the Olęders from motives of this kind must be accepted as a natural and practised thing. The litigiousness and ill will of some of the Olęder communities is moreover emphasised by an eye-witness, a perfectly well-informed witness, judge Stenger, who at the same time draws attention to the political back-cloths of the lawsuits, writing that the Olęders were driven into them by “unauthorised advisers of the secular and ecclesiastical estate”.[^103-2] It remains to examine how the economic relation of the Olęders to the manor shaped itself after the partitions. Here we must consider separately the settlements in private estates and in state estates. The number of the latter increased markedly, in consequence of the taking over by the state of church estates and of certain szlachta estates, in which Olęder settlements were particularly numerous (the Połajewo, Sieraków and Międzychód estates). In the szlachta estates the position of the Olęders improved markedly in consequence of the abolition of patrimonial jurisdiction. Undoubtedly advantageous to the Olęders was also the hostile attitude of the Prussian government towards the Polish szlachta. On the other hand, as regards the state domains, the economic situation of the Olęders deteriorated in a perfectly visible way. In all the domains they began to take from the Olęders the lands they had ploughed in, to restrict the servitude rights and to increase the labour dues. We have already adduced examples relating to the Ujście starostwo and the domain of Podstolice. Likewise the inhabitants of the Trzydomy Ol., lying on the ground of the Rogoźno and Budzyń starostwo, write in 1794 a petition to the Prussian king with the request to be kept to the obligations which they had rendered in Polish times[^103-3]. The Uścikowo Olęders in the Połajewo domain

[^103-1]: Zimmermann, I'ryderyk Wielki, II, pp. 49–82.
[^103-2]: Cf. p. 84, n. 258.
[^103-3]: AW. 'Irzydomy, C. I: „Da wir auf Allerhóchsten Beichl unsere alte Pohlnische Gerechtigkeiten haben iiberschicken missen, so haben wir erfahren, wie Allerhóchst Dieselben uns neue Lrbgerichtigkeiten allergnidigst ertheilen werden; weil aber schon eine gercume Zeit verflossen, dass wir kcine erhalten, so wagen wir es in tiefster Unterthinigkcit Euer Kónigl. Majestat, um selbige dermuthigst zu bitten, und dass Hóchst Dieselben die Gnade fur uns haben móchien, uns becy vorigen alten Dienst wie zu pohlnischen Żeiten, und bey demjenigen, was wir aufs neue zugenommen haben (nihmlich auf die halbe Hufe oder 15 pohlnische Morgen cinen Berliner Scheffel Roggen nach Kónigliche Taxa zu bezahlen, wenn wir das Erbrecht erlangen wiirden da wir bey unsern schlechten Unstimden nicht hoher steigen und mehr annehmen kónnen) allergnidigst zu Jassen: und uns die jetzt aufglegte Spanntage allerhuldreichst wieder abzuhmen und zu erlassen; auch zu erlauben, dass wir simtlichen Dienst bezahlen kónnen; und uns, da wir bey unsern Griinden keine Wiesen haben, und wir jetzt auswśrts keine Wiesen bey andern Herrschaften wie vor Zeiten geschehen, auf dem Netzbruche zur Miethe bekommen und erhalten kónnen, Wiesen uns auf Erbpacht iiberlassen wie auch freye Weide und Hiitung des Kónigl. Forst zu gestaten”. ["As we have had to submit our old Polish rights to Your Most High command, so we have learned how Your Most Highness will most graciously grant us new obligations; but since already a considerable time has elapsed without our receiving any, we venture, in deepest submission, humbly to beg Your Royal Majesty for the same, and that Your Highness may have the grace for us most graciously to grant us the former old service as in Polish times, and, as regards what we have newly taken upon ourselves (namely to pay, for the half Hufe or fifteen Polish Morgen, one Berlin scheffel of rye at the Royal valuation, should we obtain the hereditary right — since with our poor means we cannot rise higher and undertake more), most graciously to grant it; and to take away and remit most graciously the Spanntage now imposed upon us; also to permit that we may pay all services in money; and, since our lands have no meadows and we can now obtain no meadows from other lords abroad as in former times, to grant that we may obtain and receive meadows for rent on the Netzbruch, and meadows be left to us on hereditary lease, and likewise free pasture and grazing in the Royal forest be permitted to us”."]

[p. 104]

are carrying on with the State treasury lawsuits dragging on for several years[^104-1]. The State treasury is conducting a number of lawsuits also with the Olęders of the Sieraków domain; among others 1 it is suing the Olęders of Chorzępowa (Poles, as is known) for additional labour dues[^104-2]. Likewise with the Olęders of Stary and Nowy Meryń in the Międzychód domain the administration carries on numerous lawsuits throughout the whole half of the nineteenth century[^104-3]. The historical development of the manorial obligations of the Olęders was finally closed by the regulation.

G. Church obligations

Olęder settlements, from the moment of their foundation, entered within the parochial administration of the Catholic church. In connection with this, for the most part[^104-4], on a par with the other parishioners, the Olęders were obliged to pay certain dues to the parish church. Catholics and evangelicals alike were made liable to these dues. The latter had, in return, just like the Catholic population, a right to the religious ministrations of the parish priests[^104-5]. The church dues paid by the Olęders can be divided into fixed dues and dues for the individual religious services. The fixed dues were the tithe and the meszne[^104-6]. Usually either the tithe or the meszne was paid; exceptionally a certain sum was paid on account of both dues at the same time[^104-7]. Both the tithe and the meszne were paid either in grain[^104-8] or in money[^104-9]. The sources

[^104-4]: It seems that the earliest settlements were at first exempt from dues in favour of the church.

[^104-9]: AW. Huta (Knarrhitte), C. 1, fol. 21; AW. Tuchorskie Nowe Ol., A. I.

[p. 105]

also mention the kolędas[^105-1]. Incidental dues were collected for christenings, weddings, visitations, burials[^105-2]. These were the so-called iura stolae [fees for the priest's ministrations][^105-3]. The evangelical Olęders were often obliged to pay these dues also when they made use of the ministrations of evangelical clergymen[^105-4]. They were then evidently treated as compensation for the loss of church revenue. Nor was this anything exceptional; in Prussia the same was done towards Catholics[^105-5]. In many cases, when the Olęders paid the fixed dues, no dues were collected for the individual services. Most often, however, all the hitherto mentioned dues were combined into one, a certain fixed lump-sum due being established. As one established by agreement with the lord or the settlers it was sometimes called a composita[^105-6], although sometimes the composita concerned only the meszne[^105-7]. Exceptionally, for the designation of church obligations, we meet the term Gottespfennig[^105-8]. The Olęders paid the fixed dues either per unit of land, or else the whole community paid a collective due. The church obligations were defined by the lord in the charter on the basis of a previous understanding with the proper

[^105-1]: AW. Gębickie Ol., A. 1: Weddings, christenings, burials, kolędas and whatever belongs to the church, every one ought to give two złotys a year.
[^105-2]: Rel. Posn. nr 430, r. 1741, fol. 233.
[^105-3]: Cf. the charters for the Olęders of the Sieraków estate.
[^105-4]: Rel. Posn. nr 035, r. 1790, fol. 316, Ol. Słonawskie: „sie haben auch frey ihre Kinder tauffien zu lassen an welchem Ort es ihnen beliebet, wenn sie vorher dem Herrn Probst in Obornik seine Gebiihre und wass rachtmissige Satzung ist, erlegen. Item mit der 'Traung wird es eben so gehalten. Item ihre Todten mógen sie begraben dahin sie wollen”. ["they also have the freedom to have their children baptised at whatever place may please them, provided they have previously paid to the Lord Provost in Obornik his fees and whatever the lawful ordinance is. Likewise with the wedding it is held in the same way. Likewise their dead they may bury wherever they wish”]
[^105-5]: Kośc., fol. 71 and 73: The parish priest of Trzcianka in a letter of 14 February 1773 to the Prussian minister of finance writes as follows: „Die mehresten Protestanten in dieser Gegend sind vor Jahren Einkómlinge aus Deutschland gewesen, die sich auf catholischen Platzen niederge- lassen und mithin denen Juribus Stolae freywillig uniterworfen, daher es auch gekommen, als durch ihre Ausbreitung sich die Catholiken verminderten und sie durch Schwedischen Schutz Kirchen und Prediger erhielten, dass sie dennoch dennen catholischen Pfarrern einen gewissen Canon oder so genannte Compositam wie noch in Polen wo evangelische Kirchen und Prediger sind, beobachtet wird. Dass dieses kein unrechtmassiges Verfahren, wird durch die Gewohnhcit in protestantischen Lindern bewiesen, wo die ankommende Catholicken, wenn sie sich auch vermehren und catholische Priester haben, dennoch dem alten Stamm, denen protestantischen Predigern die Jura Stolae erlegen miissen, wie in Berlin, Stettin und in anderen Liindern zu schen”. ["The greater number of Protestants in this region were years ago immigrants from Germany, who settled on Catholic ground and therefore voluntarily submitted themselves to the Jura Stolae; whence it came about that, as through their spread the Catholics diminished and they obtained churches and preachers under Swedish protection, they nevertheless observed towards the Catholic parish priests a certain canon, or so-called composita, as is still observed in Poland where evangelical churches and preachers exist. That this is no unlawful procedure is proved by the custom in Protestant lands, where the Catholics arriving there, even if they increase and have Catholic priests, must nevertheless pay the Jura Stolae to the old stock, to the Protestant preachers, as is to be seen in Berlin, Stettin and other lands.”] In reply (fol. 73) the Prussian authorities argue as follows: „Der Satz, dass in protestantischen Landen die darin befindliche catholische Gemeinden, dennoch die Jura Stolae denen protestantischen Predigern erlegen massen, hier keine Anwen- dung findet, weil dort die catholische Religion blos gedułdct wird”. ["The proposition that in Protestant lands the Catholic communities dwelling therein must nevertheless pay the Jura Stolae to the Protestant preachers finds no application here, because there the Catholic religion is merely tolerated”."]
[^105-6]: AGH, Wolsztyn court, the Ruchocice estate, vol. I, fol. 95, Góździno Ol.; AGH, Śrem court, the Książ estate, vol. I, fol. 142, Kiączyńskie Ol.: the rent-paying community of the Olęders of Kiełczyn, belonging to the Książ estate, pays every year to the parish priest in Książ monetary compositas in the total amount of 19 thalers.
[^105-7]: Kl. M. Suchylas: To the Roman Catholic church they shall pay absolutely the meszne, that is, the composita settled long ago and accepted by them.
[^105-8]: AW. Niwka, A. 1.

[p. 106]

parish priest[^106-1], or else bound the Olęders to come to an understanding with the church authority in the near future. As a rule, if a settlement arose on the site of another, previously existing settlement, the obligations paid formerly were binding upon it[^106-2]. The same of course applies to settlements transferred to Olęder law without a change of the previous inhabitants[^106-3]. The manor sometimes took over entirely[^106-4], or in part[^106-5], the obligation of paying the church obligations due from the settlement, or else, by way of partial repayment of the dues, bound itself, after grain had been delivered to it, to brew gratuitously for the Olęders a certain quantity of beer[^106-6]. In other cases again the Olęders received the assurance that “they would always have the protection of the lord, so that they should give no more than what equity demands and what the church authority has ordained”. Sometimes also[^106-7] the Olęders had the obligation to pay the church obligations through the intermediacy of the manor[^106-8]. The amount of the dues shaped itself variously. The fixed dues, measured per unit of land, amounted per huba to 2–8 zł[^106-9], or a korzec of rye and a korzec of oats[^106-10]. The lump-sum due, on the other hand, did not usually exceed 50 zł[^106-11], or the corresponding quantity of grain[^106-12]; exceptionally it exceeded even 100 zł[^106-13]. One must suppose that in fixing it the decisive role was played by the area of the village and the number of settlers.

I, fol. 28) 4 wiertle of rye and 4 wiertle of oats, counting 16 garnce of the Międzychód measure to the wiertel; in the Obelżanki Ol. (Kl. XII 6 V, fol. 145) 6 korcy of rye and O korcy of oats.

[p. 107]

The Constitution of 1768 abolished the obligation of paying church dues by the dissenters. The Olęders took advantage of it[^107-1]. According to Kniat's calculations[^107-2], in 1773 per włóka of land in the rent-paying villages of the notecki district there fell: in the szlachta estates 26–66 gr, in the state estates 19 gr, sometimes indeed nothing at all was paid, in the church estates 7 thalers 42 gr Prussian as tithe. In this last case, however, the payers were almost always Catholics; besides, it is difficult here to distinguish the dues paid iure parochiali [by parochial right] from the manorial obligations. If we take into account that 1 in the rent-paying szlachta and state settlements part of the inhabitants was Catholic, there will fall to the evangelical population a quite minimal sum.

In the northern part of the Poznań county, in places where the parochial administration had been organised by the evangelical church, it also laid claim to services on the part of the Olęders. These services could have a compulsory character, just like the services in favour of the Catholic parish church[^107-3]. It even happened that the Olęders were bound to services in favour of both churches at the same time[^107-4]. The services in favour of the evangelical church sometimes reached a considerable amount[^107-5].

H. Public obligations

The Olęders, like the other peasants, were obliged to pay certain public dues. The charter for Góździn of 1735 states this as follows: And inasmuch as the Commonwealth through the sejm, or the voivodeships through the sejmiks, shall have ordained by their resolution any taxation for the new colonies, then the aforesaid Olęders shall have to comply with that resolution, having no prejudice to themselves thereby, for, dwelling in one fatherland with the others, we must bear

[^107-1]: AW. Rożnowskie Ol, B. 770: To the Reverend Father the parish priest of Rożnow, according to the old law, every one used to pay 4 zł yearly; on the foundation of the new law they will withdraw from this, so the payment of 10 is left to the further disposition of the Commonwealth in this respect. Cf. also p. 32, n. 3.
[^107-2]: Gospodarcze znaczenie ciężarów, pp. 40–1.
[^107-3]: In the Ol. Bronice every one of the settlers gave to the evangelical pastor one korzec of rye, Berlin measure.
[^107-4]: In the Ol. Meryń Nowy every one of the settlers was obliged to pay 7 tymfs both to the Catholic and to the evangelical parish priest.
[^107-5]: According to the remark in the community book of Folsztyn (AW. dep. G. 5, cf. also Schmidt, Geschichte, p. 403) the Olęders were bound to give the pastor: 1 zł per huba (part in good coin, part in szeląg coin), for every 2 hubs 1 cart of hay, a New Year offering, a tribute in eggs at Easter. Moreover, at each of the 3 feasts: Christmas, Easter and Whitsuntide, every farmer was bound to give as an offering 1½ gr. Apart from this, for the individual religious services, rather high dues were in force.

[p. 108]

the bad and the good alike”[^108-1]. There occurred, however, cases in which the lord exempted the Olęders for ever from all taxes, thereby taking upon himself the obligation of paying them out of his own purse[^108-2]. Of course the taxes could here be shifted onto the Olęders in the form of an increased rent. We meet the obligation of paying taxes already in the earliest period of the colonisation. Thus, for instance, the Nowe Dwory Ol., founded in 1601, were obliged to pay the “crown levy resolved at the sejm”[^108-3]. The same provision we find in the charters of 1677 for Folsztyn, Herbardowo and Marianowo. In the charters of the first half of the eighteenth century there appear mentions of the hearth tax[^108-4]. The Olęders in the royal estates pay the hyberna[^108-5]. The most important tax, however, from the end of the seventeenth century was the poll tax. The charters either mentioned generally the obligation of paying this tax, or recommended paying it in the amount fixed by the authorities, and then the Olęders were usually obliged to pay into the proper cash-box the amount falling to them, like the other taxes[^108-6]; or else they bound the Olęders to pay on this account a certain fixed lump sum into the lord's hands. The latter pocketed the surplus above the tariff, or else made good the difference between the tariff and the sum paid in. The fixed amount of the tax was determined per unit of land (huba), or else fixed collectively for the whole community, which apportioned it among the individual members. In the first case its average amount lay within 3–6 zł per huba, in the second it amounted to about 50 zł. Only exceptionally, in the Sieraków estate, did the poll tax exceed 100 zł[^108-7]. In connection with the fiscal reforms of 1764 and 1775[^108-8] the charters
